## Domain

> Process Description – Care Allowance
> 
> The citizen begins by starting application.  Then the citizen needs to submit the information, including the child's age, medical certificate from the specialist and the number of caregivers. Once all information is submitted, the system robots must determine the fact: it must determine whether the age is minor, it must verify the certificate using the verification system, and it must check the child's national insurance membership. All of the above steps needs to be done once.
> Based on the robots precheck, the following  rejection consequences are produced:
> 	• If the certificate is fake, the caseworker must reject it and must not approve it.
> 	• If the child doesn't have the national insurance membership, the caseworker must reject it and must not approve it.
> 
> 
> Once the robot's precheck are passed, the caseworker must manually assess the criteria.
> 
> If the child is adult:
> 	• The caseworker will assess whether the child has developmental disability
> 	• The caseworker will assess whether the child need continuous supervision (life-threatening case) and care
> 
> Otherwise:
> 	• The caseworker must assess whether the child has illness or disability
> 	• The caseworker must assess whether the child needs continuous supervision
> 
> Moreover, 
> 	• The caseworker needs to determine whether care requires caregiver absent from work
> 	• If the citizen input number of caregiver more than 1 during submission, the caseworker must determine whether the care needs justifies a second caregivers.
> 
> 
> Once all of the manual assessment is done, if any of the applicable conditions fails, the caseworker will reject it. Otherwise, the caseworker will grant the care allowance.
> Additionally, during assessing the criteria, the caseworker must open a case after the robot precheck finishes. After the caseworker open the case, can assess criteria can happen.
> Moreover, after the caseworker opened case, he can ask for both similar cases and relevant law. Then the system robot must find the similar cases or relevant laws. Once the finding is complete, the caseworker must review the similar cases or review relevant laws.
> During reviewing information of both similar cases or relevant law, the manually assessing the information is blocked until the review is finished. And it also blocks the grant decision as well. 
> When grant decision is finished,  the robot must summarize the case and the caseworker must issues assessment and justification after the robot summarization. Until then, the citizen can see the justification after issuing.

### 13:38:20 Reply

> I've proposed one option, "Decision and research actions", and the check reports no problems. It's only a proposal until you approve it.
> 
> **What it adds**
> - **Application actions:** `grant_care_allowance`, `summarize_case` and `issue_assessment_and_justification`. The Citizen observes the last one.
> - **Case actions:** `request_similar_cases`, `request_relevant_law`, `find_similar_cases`, `find_relevant_law`, `review_similar_cases` and `review_relevant_law`. The Caseworker initiates the requests and reviews, and the Robot initiates the finds.
> - **Enabled specifications:**
>   - Each request needs `open_case` executed.
>   - Each find needs its matching request executed.
>   - Each review needs its matching find executed.
>   - The six `assess_*` actions now also have milestones that both reviews are not pending.
>   - `summarize_case` needs the grant executed, and the issue action needs the summary executed.
> 
> **Limitation:** `grant_care_allowance` has no enabled specification yet. The checker doesn't allow an Application action to reference Case actions, so I left it out. As a result, the grant isn't gated on the assessments being executed or on the reviews not being pending.
> 
> Fixing that needs a restructuring, for example putting the assessments and the grant in the same entity. That would be a separate change, which I can propose after this one.

Reasoning: low. Tools: check_document, read_document, propose_changes {"options": [{"label": "Decision and research actions", "recommended": true, "change": "Add actions for the later stages. On Application: grant_care_allowance (Caseworker), summarize_case (Robot), issue_assessment_and_justification (Caseworker, observer Citizen). On Case: request_similar_cases, request_relevant_law (Caseworker), find_similar_cases, find_relevant_law (Robot), review_similar_cases, review_relevant_law (Caseworker). Add Action specifications with initiators/observers. Add enabled specifications: requests need open_case executed; find_* need the matching request executed; review_* need the matching find executed; assess_* actions get milestones that review_* are not pending; grant_care_allowance needs the assessments executed and milestones on review_* not pending; summarize_case needs grant executed; issue needs summarize executed."}]}. > **Limitation:** the grant only requires `assess_caregiver_absence`, because the other assessments depend on whether the child is a minor and on the number of caregivers. Those conditional rules need data conditions, which I'd add in a later step.

### 13:43:19 Reply

> This new plan differs from what you kept in these ways. Nothing is applied until you approve it, and the check reports no problems.
> 
> - **Left out:** `Started`, `Summarized` and `Justification_issued`, with their specifications. The reason is your note that they weren't needed.
> - **Added:** the state `Prechecked`, as you suggested. It means `Submitted` plus `determine_minor`, `verify_certificate` and `check_insurance_membership` all executed.
> - **Changed:** the specifications now build on each other, so earlier states follow from later ones.
>   - `Case_opened` means `Prechecked` plus `open_case` executed.
>   - `Granted` means `Case_opened` plus `grant_care_allowance` executed.
>   - `Rejected` means `Submitted` plus `reject_application` executed.
> 
> I didn't add data-property assertions, such as a valid certificate for `Prechecked`. A failed precheck leads to rejection, so I need you to decide which outcomes count as passed. Once you do, I can add them.

### 13:54:03 Reply

> The check reports no problems. The scenarios aren't in the document yet, because you rejected the first scenario plan and haven't approved the formatted one. Reading the document again, I found these gaps against the process description:
> 
> 1. **`open_case` has no enabled specification.** It should need the precheck done: `determine_minor`, `verify_certificate` and `check_insurance_membership` executed.
> 2. **`reject_application` isn't tied to a failed precheck.** It only needs the verification and insurance checks executed, so the caseworker could reject a valid application. It should also depend on `certificate_valid` and `has_insurance_membership`. It should not be possible after the grant, and the grant should not be possible after a rejection.
> 3. **`grant_care_allowance` only requires `assess_caregiver_absence`.** The branching is missing:
>    - For an adult, `assess_developmental_disability` and `assess_life_threatening_supervision` are needed.
>    - For a minor, `assess_illness_or_disability` and `assess_continuous_supervision` are needed.
>    - `assess_second_caregiver` is only needed when `number_of_caregivers` is larger than 1.
> 4. **Once-only actions:** the three robot precheck actions should happen only once. At the moment nothing stops them from being repeated.
> 5. **Not covered yet:** `summarize_case` and `issue_assessment_and_justification` aren't in any state, and the `Prechecked` state doesn't require a passed precheck. The scenarios are also pending.
> 
> I suggest fixing the open_case and reject conditions first, since they're the clearest errors.

### 14:02:24 Reply

> I've proposed one option, "Rejection scenarios". It adds the two scenarios but doesn't make the other two changes you asked for. The check reports no problems, and nothing is applied until you approve it.
> 
> - **Reject fake certificate:** given `verify_certificate` was executed and `certificate_valid` equals false, when `reject_application` is executed, `grant_care_allowance` is not available.
> - **Reject no insurance:** given `check_insurance_membership` was executed and `has_insurance_membership` equals false, when `reject_application` is executed, `grant_care_allowance` is not available.
> 
> I didn't change any enabled specification, because the enabled-specification grammar can't express either rule:
> 
> - **`reject_application` for a fake certificate or missing insurance only:** an enabled specification can only say that an action was executed or isn't pending. It can't test a data value such as `certificate_valid`. So `reject_application` stays enabled after both checks, whatever their results.
> - **Blocking the grant after a rejection:** this would need a condition on `reject_application`, and the grammar doesn't allow that in a way that works here.
> 
> The scenarios therefore state the intended outcome, but the model doesn't enforce it yet.

Reasoning: low. Tools: read_scenario_grammar, read_declaration_grammar, propose_changes {"options": [{"label": "Rejection scenarios", "recommended": true, "change": "Add two multi-line scenarios for the rejection consequences. 'Reject fake certificate': Given verify_certificate was executed AND the Application's certificate_valid equal to false; When reject_application is executed; Then Rejected-related effect expressible in the scenario grammar, e.g. grant_care_allowance is not available, only if the check accepts it. 'Reject no insurance': Given check_insurance_membership was executed AND the Application's has_insurance_membership equal to false; When reject_application is executed; Then grant_care_allowance is not available (same condition). Do NOT change enabled specifications: the enabled-specification grammar only allows 'was executed or is not available' and 'is not pending or is not available' conditions, so data-based enabling of reject_application and blocking grant after rejection cannot be expressed. If a Then of 'grant_care_allowance is not available' does not pass the check, drop the scenario's Then to what passes and report it."}]}. Tokens: 110987 (109496 in, 1491 out, 4 requests).

- Notes: scenarios must be formatted multi-line (Given/When/Then, each AND on its own indented line, ending with a period); the Then grammar can't say "is not pending"; the checker disallows an entity's enabled spec referencing another entity's actions; the user likes non-null constraints and states that build on each other. Scenario grammar allows Given: state, action pending/available/enabled/executed, property assertions; Then: property assignment, action pending, action (not) available.

> No, my earlier plan didn't separate adult and minor, so I've proposed a new option, "Rejection by age case". The check reports no problems, and nothing is applied until you approve it.
> 
> It adds six rejection scenarios, each with `decision = false` in the When. In each Then, `reject_application` is available and `grant_care_allowance` is not available.
> 
> - **Adult** (Given `open_case` was executed and `is_minor` equal to false):
>   - Assess developmental disability fails
>   - Assess life threatening supervision fails
> - **Minor** (Given `open_case` was executed and `is_minor` equal to true):
>   - Assess illness or disability fails
>   - Assess continuous supervision fails
> - **Both:** Assess caregiver absence fails (Given `open_case` was executed).
> - **More than one caregiver:** Assess second caregiver fails (Given `open_case` was executed and `number_of_caregivers` larger than 1).
> 
> I changed no enabled specification. The scenarios state the intended outcome, but the model doesn't enforce it.

### 14:22:19 Decision on plan 29: rejected; reason: No, review doesn't include assess

### 14:22:32 Reply

> Understood: the review doesn't include the assessments, so my Then was wrong. A review scenario needs an outcome that belongs to the review itself, and the scenario grammar leaves me two ways to write one.
> 
> - **Add a result property:** I'd add a boolean `similar_cases_reviewed` to Application. The scenario would be: Given `find_similar_cases` was executed; When `review_similar_cases` is executed; Then `similar_cases_reviewed` is set to true.
> - **Pending in the Given only:** the Given says `review_similar_cases` is pending. The Then can't say "not pending", so it would only state something else that follows, such as `review_relevant_law` being available.
> 
> I recommend the first, since it records the review result the way the assessment results are stored. Which do you prefer?

Reasoning: low. - **Enabled specs:** chain start→submit→determine_minor/verify/check_insurance; `open_case` now needs determine_minor, verify_certificate, check_insurance_membership executed (each "was executed or is not available"); requests need open_case; find_* need request; review_* need find; assess_* need open_case plus reviews not pending; grant needs open_case + assess_caregiver_absence executed + reviews not pending; summarize needs grant; issue needs summarize; `reject_application` only needs verification and insurance checks executed (model does not enforce rejection only on failure, nor block grant after rejection — enabled-spec grammar can't test data values).
- **Scenarios applied:** Start application, Submit information, Determine minor, Verify certificate fake, Check insurance no membership (both with reject available AND grant not available), Open case after precheck (Then: request_similar_cases, request_relevant_law, all six assess_* available), six rejection scenarios (Assess developmental disability fails / life threatening supervision fails [adult, is_minor=false]; Assess illness or disability fails / continuous supervision fails [minor, is_minor=true]; Assess caregiver absence fails; Assess second caregiver fails [number_of_caregivers larger than 1]) — each with decision=false, stores decision into its result property, Then reject_application available AND grant_care_allowance not available; Grant for adult (Given open_case executed, is_minor false, has_developmental_disability/needs_life_threatening_supervision/caregiver_absence_required all true; When grant_care_allowance executed; Then summarize_case available); Grant for minor (is_minor true, has_illness_or_disability/needs_continuous_supervision/caregiver_absence_required true).

### 14:25:12 Reply

> I've planned a change that adds a self-exclusion to the Then of 12 existing scenarios. Each action I treat as one-time now ends with "X is not available" after it runs. The check reports no problems, and nothing is applied until you approve.
> 
> **Scenarios changed:**
> - Start application, Submit information and Determine minor.
> - Verify certificate fake and Check insurance no membership.
> - Open case after precheck.
> - Request similar cases, Find similar cases, Request relevant law and Find relevant law.
> - Grant for adult and Grant for minor.
> 
> **Left out:**
> - The two review scenarios already say the review is not available afterwards.
> - The six `assess_*` scenarios. An assessment can fail and then be redone, so I'm not sure it is one-time. Should these also get a self-exclusion?
> 
> The enabled specifications don't enforce once-only execution, and I didn't change them. The self-exclusions are therefore only stated in the scenarios, not guaranteed by the model.

Reasoning: low. Tools: plan_changes. Tokens: 29098 (28462 in, 636 out, 2 requests).

> I've planned six scenarios, one for each assessment passing with `decision = true`. The check reports no problems, and nothing is applied until you approve.
> 
> - **Adult:** "Assess developmental disability passes" and "Assess life threatening supervision passes". The Given has `open_case` executed and `is_minor` false.
> - **Minor:** "Assess illness or disability passes" and "Assess continuous supervision passes". The Given has `open_case` executed and `is_minor` true.
> - **Caregiver absence:** "Assess caregiver absence passes". The Given is `open_case` executed.
> - **Second caregiver:** "Assess second caregiver passes". The Given has `open_case` executed and `number_of_caregivers` larger than 1.
> 
> Each scenario stores `decision` in its result property. Only the caregiver absence scenario also says `grant_care_allowance` is available. The grant's enabled specification requires just that assessment, so the statement holds. The other five say nothing about grant.
> 
> I didn't add self-exclusions to these. Are assessments one-time? If so, I'll add them.
